<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a two-dimensional ramified regular local ring <i>A</i> of mixed characteristic $`(0,5)`$ for which $`K_5(A)\to K_5(\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}\nolimits A)`$ has a nonzero kernel. This disproves Gersten's conjecture in its unrestricted integral form.

- **C1** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $V$ be the ring of integers in the unramified extension of $\mathbb Q_5$ of degree eight, and put $$A=\bigl(V[x,y]/(5+x^4+y^4)\bigr)_{(5,x,y)}.$$ Then $A$ is a Noetherian regular local domain of dimension two and $$\ker\bigl(K_5(A)\longrightarrow K_5(\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}A)\bigr)\ne0.$$ Consequently the unrestricted integral Gersten conjecture has a negative answer.
  - Explanation: Constructs a ramified regular local domain with a nonzero integral generic kernel.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-moore-product)): The spectrum $M$ has a unique unital pairing $\mu:M\wedge M\to M$ in the homotopy category. Multiplication by $5$ on $M$ is nullhomotopic, so all the coefficient groups above are $\mathbb F_5$-vector spaces. Tensor product, followed by $\mu$, defines natural bilinear products in coefficient $K$-theory and the corresponding module actions on coefficient $G$-theory.
  - Explanation: Defines coefficient products and module actions using a unique unital Moore-spectrum pairing.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bott-localization)): The operation $b$ commutes with the maps and boundary homomorphisms in the $G$-theory localization sequences over $R$, including localization at a generic point and arbitrary multiplicative localization in the Noetherian affine situations below. Under dévissage its action on a residue-field term is multiplication by the restricted class $\beta$. It is also compatible with finite coherent pushforward and flat pullback over $R$.
  - Explanation: Establishes compatibility of Bott multiplication with coherent localization, finite pushforward, and flat pullback.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-bott)): Let $H$ be a perfect field of characteristic different from $5$ containing a primitive fifth root $\zeta_H$. Suppose that $\beta_H\in K_2(H;5)$ satisfies $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta_H=[\zeta_H]$, and let $b$ denote its multiplication operation.* 1. *If $\operatorname{cd}_5H\leq3$, then $$b:K_{2r}(H;5)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_{2r+2}(H;5) \qquad(r\geq1).$$* 2. *If $\operatorname{cd}_5H\leq2$, then $$b:K_{2r+1}(H;5)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_{2r+3}(H;5) \qquad(r\geq0).$$* 3. *If $H$ is finite, then $b:K_i(H;5)\to K_{i+2}(H;5)$ is an isomorphism for every $i\geq0$.* *Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $5$, the group in degree $2j\geq0$ is generated by $b^j(1)$, and the odd-degree groups vanish. Here $1\in K_0(H;5)$ is the unit class, $b^0(1)=1$, and $b^j$ denotes iterated left multiplication.
  - Explanation: Identifies the specified Bott multiplication maps as isomorphisms under the stated field hypotheses.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-cyclotomic-unramified)): Let $N=\mathbb Q(\zeta)$ and $S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5]$ as above. If a class in $K_2(N)/5$ has zero tame residue in $k_v^\times/k_v^{\times5}$ at every closed point $v$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$, then the class is zero.
  - Explanation: Detects reductions of integral classes through tame residues away from the inverted prime.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cyclotomic-k4)): For $S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5]$, one has $K_4(S)[5]=0$.
  - Explanation: Eliminates the integral torsion obstruction to lifting the coefficient class.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-integral-lift)): Let $u=\zeta-1\in S^\times$, and let $b$ be multiplication by the class $\beta$ in <a href="#eq:equivariant-bott" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equivariant-bott">[eq:equivariant-bott]</a>. There exists $c\in K_5(S)$ such that $$\label{eq:integral-c} c\bmod5=b^2[u]_5\quad\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For every $\delta\in\Delta$, $$\label{eq:conjugate-c} (\delta c)\bmod5=\chi(\delta)^2b^2[\delta u]_5 \quad\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For any fixed homomorphism $\iota:S\to H$ to a field, let $\operatorname{res}_\iota$ denote extension of scalars. Restriction of these equalities gives the corresponding identities in $K_5(H;5)$. In the second identity this means $$\operatorname{res}_\iota(\delta c)\bmod5 =\chi(\delta)^2 b_\iota^2[\iota(\delta u)]_5,$$ where $b_\iota$ is multiplication by $\operatorname{res}_\iota(\beta)$; no action of $\delta$ on $H$ is required.
  - Explanation: Constructs an integral lift and computes its conjugates after coefficient reduction without choosing an equivariant lift.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-specialization)): Let $L$ be a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_5$ containing $\mu_5$, with valuation ring $R$ and residue field $k$. Let $\mathcal X\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension one with geometrically integral fibers $$C=\mathcal X_L,\qquad D=\mathcal X_k.$$ For a closed point $q$ of $C$, let $r(q)$ be its specialization on $D$: it is the image of the closed point under the unique extension of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\kappa(q)\to\mathcal X$ to the valuation ring of $\kappa(q)$. For a closed point $r$ of $D$, write $[r]\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(D)$ for the class of the divisor $(r)$. Let $m\geq0$ be an integer, and suppose that:* 1. *$U$ is the spectrum of an arbitrary localization of the coordinate ring of a nonempty affine open of $C$, and $p_1,\ldots,p_m$ are distinct $L$-rational closed points of $U$;* 2. *$Z$ is a finite set of closed points of $D$, and every closed point $q$ of $C$ with $r(q)\notin Z$ belongs to $U$;* 3. *$B$ is an abelian group killed by $5$, and $\psi:\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(D)\to B$ is a homomorphism satisfying $\psi([r])=0$ for every $r\in Z$.* *Put $r_i=r(p_i)\in D(k)$. Choose $\beta_L\in K_2(L;5)$ with $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta_L=[\zeta]$ for a primitive fifth root of unity $\zeta$, and let $b$ be multiplication by its restrictions, with $b^2=b\circ b$. Let $v_L$ be the normalized valuation of $L$. For $a_i\in L^\times$ and $e_i\in\mathbb F_5$, the condition $$\label{eq:specialization-test} \sum_{i=1}^m e_i v_L(a_i)\,\psi([r_i])\ne0\quad\text{in }B$$ implies $$\label{eq:specialization-conclusion} \sum_{i=1}^m (p_i)_*\bigl(e_i b^2[a_i]_5\bigr) \ne0\quad\text{in }G_5(U;5).$$ Here $[a_i]_5$ is the class of $a_i$ in $K_1(L;5)=L^\times/L^{\times5}$, and the scalars in <a href="#eq:specialization-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:specialization-test">[eq:specialization-test]</a> act through the natural $\mathbb F_5$-module structure on $B$.
  - Explanation: Detects supported coefficient classes through a Picard specialization homomorphism.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit)): There are points $Q\in E(k_0)$ and $P\in D(k)$ such that $$Q\notin5E(k),\qquad h(P)=Q,\qquad Z(P)\ne0,$$ and $$\label{eq:weighted-orbit} \begin{aligned} \sum_{a\in\mathbb F_5^\times}a^2\psi([g_aP]) &=\sum_{a\in\mathbb F_5^\times}a^2h(g_aP)\\ &=4Q\ne0\quad\text{in }E(k)/5E(k). \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Constructs an orbit whose character-weighted elliptic image remains nonzero modulo five.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-local-ring)): The ring $$\label{eq:counterexample-ring} A=\bigl(V[x,y]/(5+x^4+y^4)\bigr)_{(5,x,y)}$$ is a regular local domain of dimension two. The assignment $$\label{eq:closed-divisor-map} A\longrightarrow\mathcal O,\qquad x\longmapsto tX_0,\qquad y\longmapsto tY_0$$ is a surjective local homomorphism with nonzero height-one kernel.
  - Explanation: Constructs a regular local domain and a surjective divisor homomorphism with nonzero height-one kernel.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-splitting)): The scheme $T$ is regular, integral, Noetherian, and one-dimensional, and its morphism to $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$ is flat. The substitutions $$X/Z=x/t,\qquad Y/Z=y/t$$ identify $T$ with the spectrum of a localization of the coordinate ring of the chart $Z\ne0$ in $C$.* *The base change to $T$ of the closed immersion $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\mathcal O\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$ in <a href="#eq:closed-divisor-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:closed-divisor-map">[eq:closed-divisor-map]</a> is the disjoint union of four $L$-rational closed points $p_\delta$, indexed by $\delta\in\Delta$. Their coordinates are $$\label{eq:split-points} p_\delta: \quad (X/Z,Y/Z)= \left(\frac{\delta(t)}tX_0, \frac{\delta(t)}tY_0\right).$$ Their specializations on $\mathcal D$ are $g_{\chi(\delta)}P$. The factor indexed by $\delta$ is obtained from $\mathcal O$ by the map $a\mapsto\delta(a)$ into $L$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the flat generic curve and the distinct conjugate points of the split divisor.
- **C12** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-lem-triangular-naturality-failure)): For every regular local ring $B$ with a nonzero regular parameter $g$, the upper-left-block assignments just described fail to send all lower triangular isomorphisms between upper triangular $S_2$-objects to morphisms of the target diagrams.
  - Explanation: Exhibits a failure of strict naturality in the proposed upper-left-block assignments; this failure alone does not refute the vanishing theorem statement.

Suppose that $\\beta_H\\in K_2(H;5)$ satisfies $\\partial_{\\mathrm{coef}}\\beta_H=[\\zeta_H]$, and let $b$ denote its multiplication operation.*\n\n1.  *If $\\operatorname{cd}_5H\\leq3$, then $$b:K_{2r}(H;5)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }K_{2r+2}(H;5)\n     \\qquad(r\\geq1).$$*\n\n2.  *If $\\operatorname{cd}_5H\\leq2$, then $$b:K_{2r+1}(H;5)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }K_{2r+3}(H;5)\n     \\qquad(r\\geq0).$$*\n\n3.  *If $H$ is finite, then $b:K_i(H;5)\\to K_{i+2}(H;5)$ is an isomorphism for every $i\\geq0$.*\n\n*Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $5$, the group in degree $2j\\geq0$ is generated by $b^j(1)$, and the odd-degree groups vanish. Here $1\\in K_0(H;5)$ is the unit class, $b^0(1)=1$, and $b^j$ denotes iterated left multiplication.",
          "source_id": "PM-209.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H$ be a perfect field of characteristic different from $5$ containing a primitive fifth root $\\zeta_H$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-integral-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u=\\zeta-1\\in S^\\times$, and let $b$ be multiplication by the class $\\beta$ in <a href=\"#eq:equivariant-bott\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:equivariant-bott\">[eq:equivariant-bott]</a>. There exists $c\\in K_5(S)$ such that $$\\label{eq:integral-c}\n c\\bmod5=b^2[u]_5\\quad\\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For every $\\delta\\in\\Delta$, $$\\label{eq:conjugate-c}\n (\\delta c)\\bmod5=\\chi(\\delta)^2b^2[\\delta u]_5\n       \\quad\\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For any fixed homomorphism $\\iota:S\\to H$ to a field, let $\\operatorname{res}_\\iota$ denote extension of scalars. Restriction of these equalities gives the corresponding identities in $K_5(H;5)$. In the second identity this means $$\\operatorname{res}_\\iota(\\delta c)\\bmod5\n =\\chi(\\delta)^2 b_\\iota^2[\\iota(\\delta u)]_5,$$ where $b_\\iota$ is multiplication by $\\operatorname{res}_\\iota(\\beta)$; no action of $\\delta$ on $H$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-209.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q_5$ containing $\\mu_5$, with valuation ring $R$ and residue field $k$. Let $\\mathcal X\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension one with geometrically integral fibers $$C=\\mathcal X_L,\\qquad D=\\mathcal X_k.$$ For a closed point $q$ of $C$, let $r(q)$ be its specialization on $D$: it is the image of the closed point under the unique extension of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\kappa(q)\\to\\mathcal X$ to the valuation ring of $\\kappa(q)$. For a closed point $r$ of $D$, write $[r]\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(D)$ for the class of the divisor $(r)$. Let $m\\geq0$ be an integer, and suppose that:*\n\n1.  *$U$ is the spectrum of an arbitrary localization of the coordinate ring of a nonempty affine open of $C$, and $p_1,\\ldots,p_m$ are distinct $L$-rational closed points of $U$;*\n\n2.  *$Z$ is a finite set of closed points of $D$, and every closed point $q$ of $C$ with $r(q)\\notin Z$ belongs to $U$;*\n\n3.  *$B$ is an abelian group killed by $5$, and $\\psi:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(D)\\to B$ is a homomorphism satisfying $\\psi([r])=0$ for every $r\\in Z$.*\n\n\n\n*Put $r_i=r(p_i)\\in D(k)$. Choose $\\beta_L\\in K_2(L;5)$ with $\\partial_{\\mathrm{coef}}\\beta_L=[\\zeta]$ for a primitive fifth root of unity $\\zeta$, and let $b$ be multiplication by its restrictions, with $b^2=b\\circ b$. Let $v_L$ be the normalized valuation of $L$. For $a_i\\in L^\\times$ and $e_i\\in\\mathbb F_5$, the condition $$\\label{eq:specialization-test}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m e_i v_L(a_i)\\,\\psi([r_i])\\ne0\\quad\\text{in }B$$ implies $$\\label{eq:specialization-conclusion}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m (p_i)_*\\bigl(e_i b^2[a_i]_5\\bigr)\n       \\ne0\\quad\\text{in }G_5(U;5).$$ Here $[a_i]_5$ is the class of $a_i$ in $K_1(L;5)=L^\\times/L^{\\times5}$, and the scalars in <a href=\"#eq:specialization-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:specialization-test\">[eq:specialization-test]</a> act through the natural $\\mathbb F_5$-module structure on $B$.",
        "source_id": "PM-209.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q_5$ containing $\\mu_5$, with valuation ring $R$ and residue field $k$. For a closed point $r$ of $D$, write $[r]\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(D)$ for the class of the divisor $(r)$. Let $m\\geq0$ be an integer, and suppose that:*\n\n1.  *$U$ is the spectrum of an arbitrary localization of the coordinate ring of a nonempty affine open of $C$, and $p_1,\\ldots,p_m$ are distinct $L$-rational closed points of $U$;*\n\n2.  *$Z$ is a finite set of closed points of $D$, and every closed point $q$ of $C$ with $r(q)\\notin Z$ belongs to $U$;*\n\n3.  *$B$ is an abelian group killed by $5$, and $\\psi:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(D)\\to B$ is a homomorphism satisfying $\\psi([r])=0$ for every $r\\in Z$.*\n\n\n\n*Put $r_i=r(p_i)\\in D(k)$. Choose $\\beta_L\\in K_2(L;5)$ with $\\partial_{\\mathrm{coef}}\\beta_L=[\\zeta]$ for a primitive fifth root of unity $\\zeta$, and let $b$ be multiplication by its restrictions, with $b^2=b\\circ b$. Let $v_L$ be the normalized valuation of $L$. For $a_i\\in L^\\times$ and $e_i\\in\\mathbb F_5$, the condition $$\\label{eq:specialization-test}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m e_i v_L(a_i)\\,\\psi([r_i])\\ne0\\quad\\text{in }B$$ implies $$\\label{eq:specialization-conclusion}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m (p_i)_*\\bigl(e_i b^2[a_i]_5\\bigr)\n       \\ne0\\quad\\text{in }G_5(U;5).$$ Here $[a_i]_5$ is the class of $a_i$ in $K_1(L;5)=L^\\times/L^{\\times5}$, and the scalars in <a href=\"#eq:specialization-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:specialization-test\">[eq:specialization-test]</a> act through the natural $\\mathbb F_5$-module structure on $B$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $L$ be a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q_5$ containing $\\mu_5$, with valuation ring $R$ and residue field $k$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-orbit",
        "exact_quote": "There are points $Q\\in E(k_0)$ and $P\\in D(k)$ such that $$Q\\notin5E(k),\\qquad h(P)=Q,\\qquad Z(P)\\ne0,$$ and $$\\label{eq:weighted-orbit}\n \\begin{aligned}\n    \\sum_{a\\in\\mathbb F_5^\\times}a^2\\psi([g_aP])\n      &=\\sum_{a\\in\\mathbb F_5^\\times}a^2h(g_aP)\\\\\n      &=4Q\\ne0\\quad\\text{in }E(k)/5E(k).\n \\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-209.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "The scheme $T$ is regular, integral, Noetherian, and one-dimensional, and its morphism to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A$ is flat. The substitutions $$X/Z=x/t,\\qquad Y/Z=y/t$$ identify $T$ with the spectrum of a localization of the coordinate ring of the chart $Z\\ne0$ in $C$.*\n\n\n*The base change to $T$ of the closed immersion $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\mathcal O\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A$ in <a href=\"#eq:closed-divisor-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:closed-divisor-map\">[eq:closed-divisor-map]</a> is the disjoint union of four $L$-rational closed points $p_\\delta$, indexed by $\\delta\\in\\Delta$. Their coordinates are $$\\label{eq:split-points}\n    p_\\delta:\n    \\quad (X/Z,Y/Z)=\n    \\left(\\frac{\\delta(t)}tX_0,\n          \\frac{\\delta(t)}tY_0\\right).$$ Their specializations on $\\mathcal D$ are $g_{\\chi(\\delta)}P$.       "exact_statement": "The scheme $T$ is regular, integral, Noetherian, and one-dimensional, and its morphism to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A$ is flat. The substitutions $$X/Z=x/t,\\qquad Y/Z=y/t$$ identify $T$ with the spectrum of a localization of the coordinate ring of the chart $Z\\ne0$ in $C$.*\n\n\n*The base change to $T$ of the closed immersion $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\mathcal O\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A$ in <a href=\"#eq:closed-divisor-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:closed-divisor-map\">[eq:closed-divisor-map]</a> is the disjoint union of four $L$-rational closed points $p_\\delta$, indexed by $\\delta\\in\\Delta$. Their coordinates are $$\\label{eq:split-points}\n    p_\\delta:\n    \\quad (X/Z,Y/Z)=\n    \\left(\\frac{\\delta(t)}tX_0,\n          \\frac{\\delta(t)}tY_0\\right).$$ Their specializations on $\\mathcal D$ are $g_{\\chi(\\delta)}P$. The factor indexed by $\\delta$ is obtained from $\\mathcal O$ by the map $a\\mapsto\\delta(a)$ into $L$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Moore spectrum** ([source](#sec:coefficients)): Let $M=\mathbb S/5$ be the cofiber of multiplication by $5$ on the sphere spectrum.
- **Coefficient K-theory and G-theory** ([source](#sec:coefficients)): Our coefficient convention is $$K_i(X;5)=\pi_i(K(X)\wedge M),\qquad G_i(X;5)=\pi_i(G(X)\wedge M).$$
- **Unit coefficient class** ([source](#sec:coefficients)): For a unit $v$, the notation $[v]_5$ denotes its image in $K_1(-;5)$.
- **Codimension points** ([source](#sec:coefficients)): For a scheme $X$, the notation $X^{(j)}$ denotes its points of codimension $j$.
- **Cyclotomic arithmetic data** ([source](#sec:arithmetic)): Fix a primitive fifth root of unity $\zeta$, and put $$N=\mathbb Q(\zeta),\qquad S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5],\qquad u=\zeta-1,\qquad \Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(N/\mathbb Q).$$
- **Cyclotomic character** ([source](#sec:arithmetic)): Let $\chi:\Delta\to\mathbb F_5^\times$ be the character defined by $\delta(\zeta)=\zeta^{\chi(\delta)}$.
- **Residue fields** ([source](#sec:finite-geometry)): Set $$k_0=\mathbb F_{5^4},\qquad k=\mathbb F_{5^8}.$$
- **Fermat quartic automorphisms** ([source](#sec:finite-geometry)): For $a\in\mathbb F_5^\times$, define an automorphism of $D$ by $$g_a(X:Y:Z)=(aX:aY:Z).$$
- **Two-term elementary complexes** ([source](#pm-h-14)): We use only the objects $$(n,m)_B= \left[B^n\oplus B^m \xrightarrow{\operatorname{diag}(gI_n,I_m)}B^n\oplus B^m\right] \quad(n,m\geq0)$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-moore-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-moore-product): Moore cofiber sequence -> Natural bilinear coefficient products
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-bott) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bott): Motivic spectral sequence and the specified coefficient boundary -> Bott isomorphisms in the required degrees
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-cyclotomic-k4) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cyclotomic-k4): Brauer reciprocity, localization, geometric detection, and finite generation -> Vanishing of the integral torsion obstruction
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-integral-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-integral-lift): Coefficient exact sequence and character averaging -> Integral cyclotomic class with controlled coefficient conjugates
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-specialization): Bott untwisting, integral tame symbols, and the coniveau differential identity -> A specialization criterion for supported coefficient classes
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit): Elliptic quotient, Frobenius point count, and quadratic trace -> A nonzero weighted orbit in the elliptic quotient
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-ring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-ring): Eisenstein and Hensel arguments and regular parameters -> Ramified regular local ring with a cyclotomic closed divisor
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-splitting): Scalar extension and Galois tensor splitting -> Localized generic curve and conjugate support points
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-splitting-2): Integral coherent pushforward and flat Picard specialization -> An integral class killed generically and detected after coefficient reduction
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-triangular-naturality-failure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-triangular-naturality-failure): Encoded chain matrices and mixed triangular commuting squares -> Failure of the proposed diagram functors

          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a perfect field of characteristic different from $5$ containing a primitive fifth root $\\zeta_H$. Suppose that $\\beta_H\\in K_2(H;5)$ satisfies $\\partial_{\\mathrm{coef}}\\beta_H=[\\zeta_H]$, and let $b$ denote its multiplication operation.*\n\n1.  *If $\\operatorname{cd}_5H\\leq3$, then $$b:K_{2r}(H;5)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }K_{2r+2}(H;5)\n     \\qquad(r\\geq1).$$*\n\n2.  *If $\\operatorname{cd}_5H\\leq2$, then $$b:K_{2r+1}(H;5)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }K_{2r+3}(H;5)\n     \\qquad(r\\geq0).$$*\n\n3.  *If $H$ is finite, then $b:K_i(H;5)\\to K_{i+2}(H;5)$ is an isomorphism for every $i\\geq0$.*\n\n*Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $5$, the group in degree $2j\\geq0$ is generated by $b^j(1)$, and the odd-degree groups vanish. Here $1\\in K_0(H;5)$ is the unit class, $b^0(1)=1$, and $b^j$ denotes iterated left multiplication.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $u=\\zeta-1\\in S^\\times$, and let $b$ be multiplication by the class $\\beta$ in <a href=\"#eq:equivariant-bott\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:equivariant-bott\">[eq:equivariant-bott]</a>. There exists $c\\in K_5(S)$ such that $$\\label{eq:integral-c}\n c\\bmod5=b^2[u]_5\\quad\\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For every $\\delta\\in\\Delta$, $$\\label{eq:conjugate-c}\n (\\delta c)\\bmod5=\\chi(\\delta)^2b^2[\\delta u]_5\n       \\quad\\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For any fixed homomorphism $\\iota:S\\to H$ to a field, let $\\operatorname{res}_\\iota$ denote extension of scalars. Restriction of these equalities gives the corresponding identities in $K_5(H;5)$. In the second identity this means $$\\operatorname{res}_\\iota(\\delta c)\\bmod5\n =\\chi(\\delta)^2 b_\\iota^2[\\iota(\\delta u)]_5,$$ where $b_\\iota$ is multiplication by $\\operatorname{res}_\\iota(\\beta)$; no action of $\\delta$ on $H$ is required.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q_5$ containing $\\mu_5$, with valuation ring $R$ and residue field $k$. Let $\\mathcal X\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension one with geometrically integral fibers $$C=\\mathcal X_L,\\qquad D=\\mathcal X_k.$$ For a closed point $q$ of $C$, let $r(q)$ be its specialization on $D$: it is the image of the closed point under the unique extension of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\kappa(q)\\to\\mathcal X$ to the valuation ring of $\\kappa(q)$. For a closed point $r$ of $D$, write $[r]\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(D)$ for the class of the divisor $(r)$. Let $m\\geq0$ be an integer, and suppose that:*\n\n1.  *$U$ is the spectrum of an arbitrary localization of the coordinate ring of a nonempty affine open of $C$, and $p_1,\\ldots,p_m$ are distinct $L$-rational closed points of $U$;*\n\n2.  *$Z$ is a finite set of closed points of $D$, and every closed point $q$ of $C$ with $r(q)\\notin Z$ belongs to $U$;*\n\n3.  *$B$ is an abelian group killed by $5$, and $\\psi:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(D)\\to B$ is a homomorphism satisfying $\\psi([r])=0$ for every $r\\in Z$.*\n\n\n\n*Put $r_i=r(p_i)\\in D(k)$. Choose $\\beta_L\\in K_2(L;5)$ with $\\partial_{\\mathrm{coef}}\\beta_L=[\\zeta]$ for a primitive fifth root of unity $\\zeta$, and let $b$ be multiplication by its restrictions, with $b^2=b\\circ b$. Let $v_L$ be the normalized valuation of $L$. For $a_i\\in L^\\times$ and $e_i\\in\\mathbb F_5$, the condition $$\\label{eq:specialization-test}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m e_i v_L(a_i)\\,\\psi([r_i])\\ne0\\quad\\text{in }B$$ implies $$\\label{eq:specialization-conclusion}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m (p_i)_*\\bigl(e_i b^2[a_i]_5\\bigr)\n       \\ne0\\quad\\text{in }G_5(U;5).$$ Here $[a_i]_5$ is the class of $a_i$ in $K_1(L;5)=L^\\times/L^{\\times5}$, and the scalars in <a href=\"#eq:specialization-test\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:specialization-test\">[eq:specialization-test]</a> act through the natural $\\mathbb F_5$-module structure on $B$.",
          "source_id": "PM-209.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R5",
      "inputs": [
        "Picard detection"
      ],
      "method": "Bott untwisting, integral tame symbols, and the coniveau differential identity",
      "output": "A specialization criterion for supported coefficient classes",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-specialization"
    },

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **elliptic-count**: The smooth projective model has the stated finite-field point count, obtained from its Frobenius recurrence.
- **kernel-scope**: The constructed regular local domain has a nonzero integral generic kernel.
- **orbit-detector**: The weighted orbit is detected in the elliptic quotient rather than by forgetting its positions.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite-vs-integral**: Treat the finite-field point count as a computation of the integral kernel class. -> The finite count does not construct or verify the supported integral class.
- **rational-overreach**: Infer nonvanishing after rationalization. -> Detection of a coefficient reduction does not determine rational nonvanishing.
- **principal-pushforward**: Infer that a principal divisor has zero pushforward on higher groups. -> Self-intersection and projection identities do not establish vanishing of the pushforward.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite-field point count in the elliptic-orbit proof is constructive. This code checks that calculation; the integral lifting and specialization argument remains outside its coverage.

```python
# INPUTS: An integer m in {1, 2, 4}.
# PRECONDITIONS: The curve is the smooth projective model of w^2=s^4+1 over F_5.
# OUTPUTS: The exact number of points over F_(5^m).
# TIME: O(m) integer operations plus constant base-field enumeration.
# SPACE: O(1) integer registers; integer bit lengths grow with m.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact FLINT fmpz arithmetic, never floating point.
# FAILURE: Reject malformed inputs and extension degrees outside {1, 2, 4}.
import sys
from flint import fmpz
m = int(sys.stdin.read().strip())
if m not in (1, 2, 4):
    raise ValueError('unsupported extension degree')
base = sum(1 for s in range(5) for w in range(5) if (w*w-s**4-1)%5 == 0) + 2
trace = fmpz(6-base)
a0, a1 = fmpz(2), trace
for j in range(2, m+1):
    a0, a1 = a1, trace*a1 - 5*a0
print(fmpz(5)**m + 1 - a1)
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample concerns the specified ramified regular local domain and its integral generic restriction map. Blocked use: ['Do not infer generic noninjectivity for every regular local domain or every integral K-group.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The regular total ring is ramified and is not smooth over the base valuation ring. Blocked use: ['Do not use this example to contradict results requiring smoothness over the base valuation ring.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The example is excluded from the stated unramified mixed-characteristic regime. Blocked use: ['Do not transfer this counterexample to the unramified regular local rings covered by positive results.']
- **L4** (unknown): The detection does not determine the order of the integral kernel class or whether its rationalization is nonzero. Blocked use: ['Do not assign an order to the integral class from its nonzero coefficient reduction.', 'Do not claim a counterexample to the rational-coefficient variant.']
- **L5** (unknown): A smallest degree of failure is not determined. Blocked use: ['Do not describe the constructed degree as minimal.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): The coefficient operation uses the fixed parenthesized pairing without an assumed associative coefficient ring structure. Blocked use: ['Do not silently reassociate the coefficient products or promote this pairing to an associative ring structure.']
- **L7** (formal_scope): Compatibility with localization boundaries does not identify them with the coefficient connecting map. Blocked use: ['Do not apply localization-boundary compatibility to the coefficient connecting map without an additional argument.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The field Bott maps require a perfect field of the stated characteristic, a primitive fifth root, and the specified coefficient boundary; the degree clauses have their stated cohomological-dimension bounds. Blocked use: ['Do not use these Bott isomorphisms in residue characteristic five or outside the stated field and degree hypotheses.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Picard specialization requires the stated smooth projective model with geometrically integral fibers, the retained-point condition, and a homomorphism from the entire Picard group killing the omitted specializations. Blocked use: ['Do not infer nonvanishing from a detector defined only on selected rational points.', 'Do not omit retained-point or omitted-specialization hypotheses.']
- **L10** (edge_case): The specialization criterion gives no nonvanishing conclusion for an empty supported-point family. Blocked use: ['Do not treat the criterion as a nonvanishing assertion without its nonzero weighted Picard test.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): The surface argument uses the vanishing composite of coniveau differentials, rather than Gersten exactness on the mixed-characteristic surface. Blocked use: ['Do not introduce Gersten injectivity or exactness as an assumption of this detector.']
- **L12** (nonconstructive): The integral lift is chosen through localization surjectivity; this proof does not supply a direct map from the cyclotomic ring with five inverted to the valuation ring. Blocked use: ['Do not implement the lift as scalar extension along a nonexistent map between these rings.', 'Do not treat the existence of this higher K-theory preimage as an explicit finite algorithm.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): A principal divisor does not force its pushforward to vanish on all higher K-groups. Blocked use: ['Do not infer vanishing of the pushforward itself from the self-intersection or projection composites.']
- **L14** (counterexample_scope): The scalar forgetting calculation concerns that particular loss of signed point positions; it does not classify all scalar functionals. Blocked use: ['Do not generalize the displayed scalar cancellation to every possible scalar detector.']
- **L15** (counterexample_scope): The appendix refutes the supplied strict functor construction; its construction failure alone does not disprove the vanishing assertion. Blocked use: ['Do not claim that the appendix calculation alone refutes the theorem statement.', 'Do not make the integral kernel proof depend on the appendix.']
- **L16** (formal_scope): The target diagrams require strictly commuting natural transformations; a chain homotopy between projected composites does not supply replacement functors. Blocked use: ['Do not substitute homotopy commutativity for the strict naturality asserted in the appendix construction.']
- **L17** (formal_scope): Exact finite point counts and displayed matrix identities are executable subcalculations; their checks alone do not establish the higher K-theory kernel construction. Blocked use: ['Do not present a finite arithmetic or matrix certificate as a verification of the entire integral counterexample.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a two-dimensional ramified regular local ring <i>A</i> of mixed characteristic $`(0,5)`$ for which $`K_5(A)\to K_5(\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}\nolimits A)`$ has a nonzero kernel. This disproves Gersten's conjecture in its unrestricted integral form.

<div class="center">

`An-Integral-Counterexample-to-Gerstens-Conjecture-September-25-2026`

</div>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a Noetherian regular local domain $A$ with fraction field $F$, Gersten’s conjecture asserts exactness of the canonical augmented residue complex <a id="eq:gersten"></a>$$\label{eq:gersten}
 0\longrightarrow K_n(A)\longrightarrow K_n(F)
 \longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\operatorname{ht}\mathfrak p=1}
 K_{n-1}(\kappa(\mathfrak p))\longrightarrow\cdots .$$ Here $n\geq0$, the groups are integral Quillen $K$-groups, and the residues come from localization and the codimension filtration. The first assertion is generic injectivity: a class that vanishes after passage to the fraction field must already vanish on $A$. We construct a counterexample to this assertion.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $V$ be the ring of integers in the unramified extension of $\mathbb Q_5$ of degree eight, and put $$A=\bigl(V[x,y]/(5+x^4+y^4)\bigr)_{(5,x,y)}.$$ Then $A$ is a Noetherian regular local domain of dimension two and $$\ker\bigl(K_5(A)\longrightarrow K_5(\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}A)\bigr)\ne0.$$ Consequently the unrestricted integral Gersten conjecture has a negative answer.*

</div>

The maximal ideal of $A$ is $(x,y)$, and $5\in(x,y)^4$. Its special fiber has dimension one and embedding dimension two at the closed point. Thus the regular total ring is ramified and is not smooth over $V$. This distinction is central both to the example and to its relation with positive results on Gersten’s conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### History and related results.

Gersten’s questions about transfer and local higher $K$-theory (Gersten 1973a) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Gersten1973Problems](#ref-Gersten1973Problems)], together with his work on exact sequences (Gersten 1973b) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Gersten1973Exact](#ref-Gersten1973Exact)], place the conjecture in the study of localization and residue maps. Quillen formulated it for arbitrary regular local rings and proved it for semilocalizations of finite-type algebras over a field at regular primes (Quillen 1973b, sec. 7, Conjecture 5.10 and Theorem 5.11) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Quillen1973Higher](#ref-Quillen1973Higher)]. Panin established the full equicharacteristic case (Panin 2003, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Panin2003](#ref-Panin2003)].

In mixed characteristic, Gillet and Levine’s relative theorem reduces the smooth local case to the associated discrete valuation ring case (Gillet and Levine 1987) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GilletLevine1987](#ref-GilletLevine1987)]. Geisser and Levine prove finite-coefficient Gersten exactness for semilocal rings essentially smooth over a discrete valuation ring, for every finite coefficient order, including powers of the residue characteristic (Geisser and Levine 2000, Theorem 8.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GeisserLevine2000](#ref-GeisserLevine2000)]; their text credits B. Kahn for pointing out this consequence. Skalit proved finite-coefficient Gersten exactness for arbitrary unramified regular local rings, again for every finite coefficient order (Skalit 2017, Corollary 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Skalit2017](#ref-Skalit2017)]. In mixed characteristic $(0,p)$, unramified means $p\notin\mathfrak m^2$; our ring is excluded since $5\in\mathfrak m^4$. Version 1 of Druzhinin’s preprint states a result for essentially smooth local Henselian schemes over a discrete valuation ring (Druzhinin 2025, Theorem 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Druzhinin2025](#ref-Druzhinin2025)]. The smoothness hypotheses exclude the structure map $V\to A$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

Feld’s finite-coefficient example is a close methodological predecessor (Feld 2026, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Feld2026](#ref-Feld2026)]. It gives a degree-two augmentation kernel modulo three at a ramified regular local ring whose integral $K_2$ map is injective. Its supported pushforward, character averaging, cyclotomic scalar extension, conjugate splitting and divisor-class detection also organize our construction. In that example, an integral lift of the coefficient kernel has nonzero but three-divisible generic image, as follows from integral injectivity and the coefficient exact sequence. An integral generic kernel therefore requires an additional argument. Here a vanishing result for integral $K_4$ permits a cyclotomic coefficient class to lift to integral $K_5$. Two Bott factors contribute the square of the cyclotomic character, whose signs are realized by an elliptic quotient of the Fermat quartic.

Our companion paper, *An integral degree-three Gersten counterexample* (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[OpenAIDegreeThree2026](#ref-OpenAIDegreeThree2026)], constructs an integral $K_3$ kernel at a different ramified ring: the local ring at the special point $[0:0:1]$ of the cyclotomic quintic model $(X-Z)^5-XY^4+Y^5+\zeta Z^5=0$ over the integers of $\mathbb Q_5(\zeta)$, where $\zeta$ is a primitive fifth root of unity. Its proof uses a Steinberg relation and a norm valuation test on coherent $K$-theory (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 4.2, Proposition 3.1, and §5) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAIDegreeThree2026](#ref-OpenAIDegreeThree2026)]. Here the global arithmetic lift, smooth Picard specialization criterion and quartic construction are proved directly. Neither proof uses results of the other paper, and neither determines a smallest degree of failure.

Our proof detects the reduction modulo five of an integral class already killed over the fraction field. It therefore also gives generic noninjectivity with coefficients $\mathbb Z/5$ for the same ring. This detection does not determine the order of the integral class or whether its rationalization is nonzero. The conclusion concerns the unrestricted integral conjecture and does not settle its rational-coefficient variant.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Construction and proof guide.

A class pushed forward from a closed divisor of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$ vanishes over $\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}A$. We must construct such a class integrally and prove that its pushforward is nonzero. These tasks account for the two branches of the proof.

For the integral construction, put $S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5]$. Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">2</a> supplies coefficient multiplication and its exact field isomorphisms at the low degrees used here. Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">3</a> chooses $\beta\in K_2(S;5)$, writes $b$ for left multiplication by $\beta$, and constructs $$c\in K_5(S),\qquad c\bmod5=b^2[\zeta-1]_5,$$ where $b^2(x)=\beta\cdot(\beta\cdot x)$ and $[v]_5$ is the coefficient $K_1$-class of a unit. The obstruction is $K_4(S)[5]$, the subgroup killed by five. Brauer reciprocity, geometric coefficient detection and finite generation prove that this subgroup vanishes. Conjugating the reduction of $c$ multiplies its two Bott factors by the square of the cyclotomic character.

Write $H_0=\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}V$, $L=H_0(\zeta)$, and let $\mathcal O$ be the valuation ring of $L$. Integral localization supplies the following route: $$c\in K_5(S)\longmapsto c_L\in K_5(L)
 \longleftarrow\widetilde c\in K_5(\mathcal O).$$ The leftward arrow is restriction from $\mathcal O$, and $\widetilde c$ is a chosen preimage; its existence uses integral finite-field $K_4$ vanishing. Section <a href="#sec:local-geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local-geometry">6</a> constructs a surjection $A\to\mathcal O$. Its closed immersion $i$ then gives the integral class $\gamma=i_*\widetilde c\in K_5(A)$, with zero generic restriction.

For detection, flat extension from $V$ to $L$ splits this divisor into four rational points on the generic Fermat quartic $X^4+Y^4=Z^4$. Section <a href="#sec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:specialization">4</a> proves a general criterion on an arbitrary localization of a smooth affine curve. A hypothetical relation among supported coefficient classes untwists, by two Bott operations, to ordinary integral tame symbols. On a smooth proper arithmetic surface, the coniveau identity $d_1^2=0$ makes their horizontal specialization a principal divisor. A homomorphism from the entire special-fiber Picard group can therefore test the proposed relation.

Section <a href="#sec:finite-geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-geometry">5</a> constructs this test. The Fermat quartic maps with degree two to the genus-one curve $w^2=s^4+1$. With a branch point as origin, the four chosen specializations map to $Q$ or $-Q$, with the same signs as the character square. Their weighted sum is $4Q\ne0$ modulo five. Section <a href="#sec:main-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:main-proof">7</a> verifies all hypotheses of the Picard criterion and proves that the coefficient reduction of $\gamma$ remains nonzero after flat pullback. This detects the already integral class $\gamma$.

Appendix <a href="#app:claimed-vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:claimed-vanishing">8</a> treats a separate issue in Mochizuki’s version 8 preprint (Mochizuki 2020) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Mochizuki2020](#ref-Mochizuki2020)]. Its Theorem 5 would annihilate the principal-divisor pushforward used here. We prove an explicit failure of the strict functor assertion in Lemma 13(III), for every nonzero regular parameter. That calculation concerns the supplied proof construction, and by itself does not refute its theorem statement. No argument in the kernel construction depends on the appendix.

<a id="sec:coefficients"></a>
### Coefficients and Bott multiplication

Our first goal is to control multiplication by a chosen coefficient class, including its interaction with localization. These operations have two uses. In the arithmetic construction they carry surjectivity of a valuation map to the degree needed to remove the integral lifting obstruction. In the geometric argument they translate a relation among supported coefficient classes into ordinary tame symbols.

We write $G(X)$ for the $K$-theory spectrum of coherent modules on a Noetherian scheme $X$. For the regular schemes used below, the canonical resolution equivalence identifies $K(X)$ with $G(X)$: these schemes have finite dimension and are affine or quasiprojective over an affine base. We use Quillen localization and dévissage, including localization by a multiplicative set and the resulting residue maps (Quillen 1973b, sec. 4, Theorem 3 and Corollary 2; §5, Theorems 4–5; §7, Proposition 3.2 and paragraph 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Quillen1973Higher](#ref-Quillen1973Higher)].

Let $M=\mathbb S/5$ be the cofiber of multiplication by $5$ on the sphere spectrum. Our coefficient convention is $$K_i(X;5)=\pi_i(K(X)\wedge M),\qquad
 G_i(X;5)=\pi_i(G(X)\wedge M).$$ For $i\geq1$ there is a natural exact sequence <a id="eq:coefficient-sequence"></a>$$\label{eq:coefficient-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow K_i(X)/5\longrightarrow K_i(X;5)
 \xrightarrow{\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}}K_{i-1}(X)[5]
 \longrightarrow0,$$ and likewise for $G$. Here $A/5=A/5A$ and $A[5]=\ker(5:A\to A)$ for any abelian group $A$. If $H$ is a field, the identities $K_1(H)=H^\times$ and $K_0(H)=\mathbb Z$ give <a id="eq:low-coefficients"></a>$$\label{eq:low-coefficients}
 K_1(H;5)=H^\times/H^{\times5},\qquad K_0(H;5)=\mathbb Z/5.$$ For a unit $v$, the notation $[v]_5$ denotes its image in $K_1(-;5)$. We use additive notation in $K$-groups, including groups of unit classes. For a scheme $X$, the notation $X^{(j)}$ denotes its points of codimension $j$.

We make the coefficient product explicit so that localization acts on classes with nonzero coefficient boundary as well as on reductions of integral classes. At the prime five this agrees with the product in (Friedlander and Suslin 2002, Proposition 16.1 and Theorem 16.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[FriedlanderSuslin2002](#ref-FriedlanderSuslin2002)].

<a id="stmt-lem-moore-product"></a>
<div id="lem:moore-product" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Coefficient products). *The spectrum $M$ has a unique unital pairing $\mu:M\wedge M\to M$ in the homotopy category. Multiplication by $5$ on $M$ is nullhomotopic, so all the coefficient groups above are $\mathbb F_5$-vector spaces. Tensor product, followed by $\mu$, defines natural bilinear products in coefficient $K$-theory and the corresponding module actions on coefficient $G$-theory.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-moore-product"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-moore-product</summary>

*Proof.* The cofiber sequence for $M$, together with $\pi_1\mathbb S=\pi_2\mathbb S=\mathbb Z/2$, gives $\pi_1M=\pi_2M=0$. Applying maps into $M$ to this cofiber sequence shows that restriction along the unit $\eta:\mathbb S\to M$ is injective on $$[M,M]\longrightarrow[\mathbb S,M]=\mathbb Z/5.$$ Thus $5\operatorname{id}_M=0$. The cofiber sequence $$M\xrightarrow{5}M\xrightarrow{\eta\wedge1}M\wedge M
 \longrightarrow\Sigma M$$ therefore permits an extension of $\operatorname{id}_M$ to a pairing $\mu$. Its restriction along $1\wedge\eta$ is also the identity: these endomorphisms of $M$ agree after restriction along $\eta$. Moreover, $[\Sigma M,M]=0$, by the same cofiber sequence and the two vanishing homotopy groups. The extension is consequently unique. Smashing $5\operatorname{id}_M=0$ with any spectrum proves the assertion about coefficients. The products are induced by the usual tensor pairings of $K$-theory spectra and this fixed pairing on $M$. ◻

</details>

For a base ring $R$ and a class $\beta\in K_2(R;5)$, let $$b: G_i(X;5)\longrightarrow G_{i+2}(X;5)$$ be left multiplication by the restriction of $\beta$, whenever $X$ is an $R$-scheme. We use the same notation for $K$-groups, and write $b^2=b\circ b$. In particular $b^2(x)=\beta\cdot(\beta\cdot x)$, with these parentheses. We use this fixed two-factor pairing without assuming an associative coefficient ring structure.

<a id="stmt-lem-bott-localization"></a>
<div id="lem:bott-localization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Compatibility with localization). *The operation $b$ commutes with the maps and boundary homomorphisms in the $G$-theory localization sequences over $R$, including localization at a generic point and arbitrary multiplicative localization in the Noetherian affine situations below. Under dévissage its action on a residue-field term is multiplication by the restricted class $\beta$. It is also compatible with finite coherent pushforward and flat pullback over $R$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bott-localization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bott-localization</summary>

*Proof.* Tensoring with a finitely generated projective $R$-module is exact on coherent modules and preserves every specified support. Tensor product therefore gives compatible pairings by $K(R)$ on the supported, whole, and localized categories. These pairings respect the null composite in the localization diagram and hence its cofiber sequence. On a dévissage category, tensoring is tensoring with the restriction to the residue field. The projection formula for a finite map and the usual flat base-change identity are identities of these tensor functors. For each term $B$ of this localization triangle, use the pairing $$(K(R)\wedge M)\wedge(B\wedge M)
 \longrightarrow K(R)\wedge B\wedge M\wedge M
 \longrightarrow B\wedge M,$$ where the last arrow uses the integral tensor action and the fixed pairing on $M$. These pairings commute with all arrows of the triangle. Precomposing with the representative $\beta:\mathbb S^2\to K(R)\wedge M$ gives the asserted diagrams for $b$; no integral lift of $\beta$ is required. Its degree is even, so moving it across a boundary contributes no sign. Passage to a generic point follows by filtered localization. These assertions concern localization boundaries; the coefficient connecting map $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}$ is a different map. ◻

</details>

We will use the following precise instances of field Bott periodicity. They concern multiplication by the specified coefficient class, rather than an unspecified isomorphism between the groups.

<a id="stmt-lem-bott"></a>
<div id="lem:bott" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (The field Bott maps). *Let $H$ be a perfect field of characteristic different from $5$ containing a primitive fifth root $\zeta_H$. Suppose that $\beta_H\in K_2(H;5)$ satisfies $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta_H=[\zeta_H]$, and let $b$ denote its multiplication operation.*

1.  *If $\operatorname{cd}_5H\leq3$, then $$b:K_{2r}(H;5)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_{2r+2}(H;5)
     \qquad(r\geq1).$$*

2.  *If $\operatorname{cd}_5H\leq2$, then $$b:K_{2r+1}(H;5)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_{2r+3}(H;5)
     \qquad(r\geq0).$$*

3.  *If $H$ is finite, then $b:K_i(H;5)\to K_{i+2}(H;5)$ is an isomorphism for every $i\geq0$.*

*Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic different from $5$, the group in degree $2j\geq0$ is generated by $b^j(1)$, and the odd-degree groups vanish. Here $1\in K_0(H;5)$ is the unit class, $b^0(1)=1$, and $b^j$ denotes iterated left multiplication.*

</div>

The arithmetic and geometric arguments use the following instances of the lemma. For number fields and finite extensions of $\mathbb Q_5$, the $5$-cohomological dimension is two; see (Weibel 2013, VI.§7 and VI.8.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. For a one-variable function field over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_5$, the cohomological-dimension bound (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Lemma 59.95.4, Tag `0F0T`) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject)] gives $5$-cohomological dimension at most $2+1=3$. In particular, the actual multiplication maps are <a id="eq:bott-maps-used"></a>$$\label{eq:bott-maps-used}
 \begin{aligned}
  b^2 &:K_1(H;5)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_5(H;5)
    &&\text{for the number and local fields used below},\\
  b^2 &:K_2(J;5)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }K_6(J;5)
    &&\text{for the function field of the generic curve}.
 \end{aligned}$$ In each case the primitive root and the Bott class are obtained by restriction from the indicated base. For a finite field $H$ satisfying the lemma’s hypotheses, its finite-field clause also gives $b^2:K_0(H;5)\xrightarrow{\sim}K_4(H;5)$. In the arithmetic construction this will raise the target of the valuation homomorphism from degree zero to degree four.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-bott"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-bott</summary>

*Proof of Lemma <a href="#lem:bott" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott">4</a>.* We use the corrected, strongly convergent motivic spectral sequence with its finite-coefficient multiplicative structure: <a id="eq:motivic-ss"></a>$$\label{eq:motivic-ss}
 E_2^{p,q}=H^{p-q}(H,\mathbb Z/5(-q))
 \Longrightarrow K_{-p-q}(H;5).$$ This form of the spectral sequence is recorded in (Weibel 2013, 145:VI.4.2, Addendum VI.4.2.1, and VI.4.4) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. For the corrected construction, see (A. A. Suslin 2003, Introduction and Theorem 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Suslin2003](#ref-Suslin2003)] (the locators use the *Trudy* pagination). The product is the usual $K$-theory product on the abutment and the motivic cup product on $E_2$ (Friedlander and Suslin 2002, Proposition 16.1 and Theorem 16.2) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FriedlanderSuslin2002](#ref-FriedlanderSuslin2002)]. The Moore-space comultiplication used there corresponds by stable duality to a unital pairing on $M$. Its coefficient product agrees with ours by the uniqueness in Lemma <a href="#lem:moore-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:moore-product">2</a>.

Write an $E_2$-term as $H^s(H,\mathbb Z/5(j))$, of total $K$-degree $2j-s$. Terms of negative weight vanish. For $j\geq0$, the defining motivic complex $C_*\mathbb Z_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb G_m^{\wedge j})[-j]$ is concentrated in cohomological degrees at most $j$. Global sections on the Nisnevich site of a field are exact, since every covering of the field point has a splitting, and derived reduction modulo five does not increase this upper degree. Thus the field motivic group vanishes when $s>j$. For $s\leq j$, the norm-residue and Beilinson–Lichtenbaum comparison theorems identify it with <a id="eq:motivic-etale"></a>$$\label{eq:motivic-etale}
 H^s_{\mathrm{et}}(H,\mu_5^{\otimes j})$$ (Voevodsky 2011, Theorems 6.16–6.17) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Voevodsky2011](#ref-Voevodsky2011)]. These are the Rost–Voevodsky norm-residue theorem and its motivic comparison consequence, with the completion of the norm-residue argument in (Weibel 2009) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Weibel2009](#ref-Weibel2009)]; the earlier motivic formulation is (A. Suslin and Voevodsky 2000) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[SuslinVoevodsky2000](#ref-SuslinVoevodsky2000)]. In particular the terms of negative cohomological degree vanish. Under the dimension bounds below, only finitely many terms contribute to each total degree, so the resulting filtrations are finite. In these coordinates a differential $d_\rho$, for $\rho\geq2$, goes from $(s,j)$ to $(s+2\rho-1,j+\rho-1)$.

We first derive the algebraically closed case from this same spectral sequence. If $\overline H$ is an algebraic closure of $H$, only the terms with $s=0$ survive. In each degree $2j\geq0$ there is exactly one term, $H^0(\overline H,\mu_5^{\otimes j})\cong\mathbb F_5$, and in odd degree there is none. There are no possible differentials. Thus the edge map is an isomorphism in every even degree, and $$K_{2j}(\overline H;5)\cong\mathbb F_5,\qquad
 K_{2j+1}(\overline H;5)=0\qquad(j\geq0).$$ The coefficient sequence surjects $K_2(\overline H;5)$ onto $\overline H^\times[5]=\mu_5(\overline H)$. Both groups have dimension one, so this boundary is an isomorphism. Consequently the restriction of $\beta_H$ is nonzero, and so is its edge image. Multiplicativity now shows that each $b^j(1)$ over $\overline H$, computed by iterated left multiplication, has nonzero edge image and generates the corresponding even group. This proves the last assertion of the lemma without a separate rigidity input.

By naturality, the edge image $\alpha\in H^0(H,\mu_5)$ of $\beta_H$ is nonzero: restriction on this $H^0$ group is injective, and its geometric image is the nonzero class just computed. The edge image of $b^r(1)$ is $\alpha^{\otimes r}$. Since $H$ contains $\mu_5$, this generates the one-dimensional group $H^0(H,\mu_5^{\otimes r})$. Hence this whole line consists of permanent cycles for every $r\geq1$. Only $\alpha\ne0$ is needed; no choice of an identification of $\alpha$ with the specified primitive root is required.

Suppose first that $\operatorname{cd}_5H\leq3$. In total degree $2r$, the only possible terms are $$H^0(H,\mu_5^{\otimes r}),\qquad
 H^2(H,\mu_5^{\otimes(r+1)}).$$ There is no incoming differential to either term. The only possible outgoing differential from the first is $d_2$ to an $H^3$-term, and it vanishes by permanence of the Bott power. Every outgoing differential from the second has cohomological degree at least $5$ and is zero. We obtain a two-step filtration, expressed as <a id="eq:even-field-filtration"></a>$$\label{eq:even-field-filtration}
 0\longrightarrow H^2(H,\mu_5^{\otimes(r+1)})
 \longrightarrow K_{2r}(H;5)
 \longrightarrow H^0(H,\mu_5^{\otimes r})
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Multiplication by $\beta_H$ preserves these filtrations and acts on both graded pieces by tensoring with $\alpha$. Each such map is an isomorphism, proving (1).

If $\operatorname{cd}_5H\leq2$, the sole term in total degree $2r+1$ is $H^1(H,\mu_5^{\otimes(r+1)})$, with no possible nonzero differential. Multiplication by $\beta_H$ again acts by tensoring with $\alpha$. This proves (2). For a finite field, the same calculation applies in every positive degree since its $5$-cohomological dimension is one. In degree zero, the class $1$ generates $K_0(H;5)$ and $b(1)=\beta_H$ generates $K_2(H;5)=H^0(H,\mu_5)$. This proves (3). ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:arithmetic"></a>
### An integral cyclotomic class

The coefficient exact sequence identifies the obstruction to lifting a degree-five coefficient class: it is a class in integral $K_4$ killed by five. We will show that this obstruction vanishes for one cyclotomic ring. The proof first uses localization to detect coefficient $K_4$ geometrically, then uses finite generation to eliminate all five-primary torsion. We finish by recording how the chosen integral lift behaves under the four cyclotomic automorphisms.

Fix a primitive fifth root of unity $\zeta$, and put $$N=\mathbb Q(\zeta),\qquad S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5],\qquad
 u=\zeta-1,\qquad \Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{Gal}}(N/\mathbb Q).$$ Let $\chi:\Delta\to\mathbb F_5^\times$ be the character defined by $\delta(\zeta)=\zeta^{\chi(\delta)}$. We use the coefficient convention and product of Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### The cyclotomic ring.

We record the arithmetic facts that will control the localization sequence. Let $\Phi_5(X)=X^4+X^3+X^2+X+1$ be the fifth cyclotomic polynomial. The polynomial of $u$ is $$\Phi_5(1+T)=T^4+5T^3+10T^2+10T+5,$$ which is Eisenstein at five. In particular it is irreducible over $\mathbb Q_5$, so $N$ has exactly one place above five. The ring $\mathbb Z_5[u]$ is finite free over $\mathbb Z_5$; irreducibility makes it a domain, and integrality gives dimension one. Its reduction modulo five is $\mathbb F_5[T]/(T^4)$, with unique maximal ideal generated by $T$. Thus $\mathbb Z_5[u]$ is a Noetherian local domain with maximal ideal $(5,u)$. The defining equation gives $$u^4=-5(1+2u+2u^2+u^3).$$ The factor in parentheses is a unit, since its residue modulo $(5,u)$ is one. Hence $(5,u)=(u)$, and $\mathbb Z_5[u]$ is a discrete valuation ring. Being integrally closed with fraction field $\mathbb Q_5(u)$, it is the integer ring of that field, with residue field $\mathbb F_5$ and ramification index four.

The norm of $u$ is $\Phi_5(1)=5$. Since $\Phi_5'(\zeta)=5\zeta^4/(\zeta-1)$, the discriminant of the power basis $1,\zeta,\zeta^2,\zeta^3$ is $$\operatorname{N}_{N/\mathbb Q}\bigl(\Phi_5'(\zeta)\bigr)
 =\frac{5^4}{\operatorname{N}_{N/\mathbb Q}(u)}=125.$$ Let $\mathcal O_N$ be the ring of integers of $N$. The index of $\mathbb Z[\zeta]$ in $\mathcal O_N$ can therefore have no prime divisor other than five, by the discriminant-index formula. The local calculation above shows that this order is already maximal at five. Thus the index is one: $$\mathcal O_N=\mathbb Z[\zeta],\qquad \operatorname{disc}(N)=125.$$ There are two complex places and no real places. Minkowski’s ideal-class bound (Milne 2020a, chap. 4, Theorem 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[MilneANT2020](#ref-MilneANT2020)] is consequently $$\frac{4!}{4^4}\left(\frac4\pi\right)^2\sqrt{125}<2.$$ Every ideal class has an integral ideal representative of positive integral norm at most this bound, hence of norm one. It follows that $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathcal O_N)=0$, and localization gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(S)=0$.

The norm calculation also shows that $u$ and all its conjugates are units in $S$. Every closed point $v$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$ has a finite residue field $k_v$ of characteristic different from five. The image of $\zeta$ in $k_v$ has order exactly five: its order divides five, and it cannot be one because $u$ is a unit. These are precisely the residue fields to which we will apply the finite-field clause of Lemma <a href="#lem:bott" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### An equivariant coefficient class.

<a id="eq:equivariant-bott"></a>
The determinant splits the unit homomorphism $S^\times\to K_1(S)$, so $[\zeta]\in K_1(S)$ has order five. The coefficient exact sequence supplies $\beta'\in K_2(S;5)$ with $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta'=[\zeta]$. This coefficient group is an $\mathbb F_5$-vector space by Lemma <a href="#lem:moore-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:moore-product">2</a>, and $\Delta$ acts on it by functoriality. Define $$e_\chi=\frac14\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}\chi(\delta)^{-1}\delta
       \in\mathbb F_5[\Delta],\qquad \beta=e_\chi\beta'.$$ Here $1/4$ is the inverse of $4$ in $\mathbb F_5$. Naturality makes the coefficient boundary $\Delta$-equivariant, and $\delta[\zeta]=\chi(\delta)[\zeta]$. Hence $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta=e_\chi[\zeta]=[\zeta]$. Relabeling the sum gives $\tau e_\chi=\chi(\tau)e_\chi$ for every $\tau\in\Delta$, so $$\label{eq:equivariant-bott}
 \partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta=[\zeta],\qquad
 \delta\beta=\chi(\delta)\beta\quad(\delta\in\Delta).$$ For any $S$-scheme on which the operation is used, let $b$ denote left multiplication by the restriction of $\beta$, and put $b^2=b\circ b$. Thus $b^2(x)=\beta\cdot(\beta\cdot x)$, with the indicated parentheses. The coefficient localization boundaries below are different from $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}$; Lemma <a href="#lem:bott-localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott-localization">3</a> states that $b$ commutes with the former.

The next lemma is the arithmetic reason that localization will leave only a single coefficient line. It concerns reductions of integral $K_2$-classes, not all of $K_2(N;5)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-cyclotomic-unramified"></a>
<div id="lem:cyclotomic-unramified" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Unramified degree-two classes). *Let $N=\mathbb Q(\zeta)$ and $S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5]$ as above. If a class in $K_2(N)/5$ has zero tame residue in $k_v^\times/k_v^{\times5}$ at every closed point $v$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$, then the class is zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cyclotomic-unramified"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cyclotomic-unramified</summary>

*Proof.* Matsumoto’s symbol theorem (Matsumoto 1969, sec. 5, Theorem 5.10 and Corollary 5.11) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Matsumoto1969](#ref-Matsumoto1969)] gives the classical Steinberg-symbol presentation; see (Weibel 2013, 145:III.6.1) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)] for its modern formulation. The classical-to-Quillen comparison (Weibel 2013, 145:IV.1.10.1 and IV.7.2) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)] identifies this group with $K_2(N)$. The degree-two norm-residue theorem (Voevodsky 2011, Theorem 6.16) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Voevodsky2011](#ref-Voevodsky2011)] then gives $$K_2(N)/5\simeq H^2(N,\mu_5^{\otimes2}).$$ Use $\zeta$ to identify one copy of $\mu_5$ with the constant module $\mathbb Z/5$. The Kummer sequence identifies the resulting group $H^2(N,\mu_5)$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Br}}(N)[5]$. Write $a$ for the Brauer class corresponding to the class in the lemma.

For a closed point $v$ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$, the residue characteristic is not five. The Quillen boundary on $K_2(N)$ is the tame symbol. Under norm residue and the chosen twist identification, its reduction modulo five and the Brauer residue agree up to an overall sign depending on boundary conventions; in particular, their vanishing is equivalent. Indeed, write each field element as a power of a uniformizer times a unit. Both residues kill unit–unit symbols, and on a symbol consisting of a unit $\epsilon$ and a uniformizer they give the class of $\bar\epsilon$, up to the corresponding sign. Bilinearity and the Steinberg relations give the general case (Weibel 2013, vol. 145, v. 6.1.2 and V.(6.6)) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)] <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Harari2023Brauer](#ref-Harari2023Brauer)] (Harari 2023, Proposition 7.10). Thus $a$ has zero Brauer residue at every finite place $v\nmid5$.

The completion $N_v$ has finite residue field $k_v$. For its henselian valuation ring, the kernel of the Brauer residue is $\mathop{\mathrm{Br}}(k_v)=0$ (Harari 2023, Proposition 6.7 and Example 6.8(a)) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Harari2023Brauer](#ref-Harari2023Brauer)]. Consequently $a|_{N_v}=0$ at every $v\nmid5$. The infinite completions are complex and have zero Brauer group. Only the single place above five remains. In the global Brauer invariant sequence, the sum of all local invariants is zero, so the invariant at this last place is zero. The local invariant is injective there, and the global restriction map is injective. These are the local and global Brauer statements in (Milne 2020b, IV, Example 2.14(c)–(e), Proposition 4.3 and Remark 4.4(a)) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[MilneCFT2020](#ref-MilneCFT2020)]; see also (Weibel 2013, 145:III.6 and VI.8.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. They imply $a=0$, hence the original class is zero. No tame-residue calculation at the place above five is used. ◻

</details>

We can now eliminate the obstruction in integral $K_4$. The first part of the proof detects coefficient classes after passage to an algebraic closure; the last part explains why this also rules out integral five-primary torsion.

<a id="stmt-lem-cyclotomic-k4"></a>
<div id="lem:cyclotomic-k4" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (The integral torsion obstruction vanishes). *For $S=\mathbb Z[\zeta,1/5]$, one has $K_4(S)[5]=0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cyclotomic-k4"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cyclotomic-k4</summary>

*Proof.* Because $S$ is a Dedekind ring with $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(S)=0$, every divisor on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$ is principal. Equivalently, the normalized valuation map $$N^\times\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{v\in(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S)^{(1)}}\mathbb Z,
 \qquad f\longmapsto\bigl(v(f)\bigr)_v,$$ is surjective. The sums here and below have finite support. Quillen localization and dévissage for $S$ (Quillen 1973b, sec. 7, Proposition 3.2 and paragraph 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Quillen1973Higher](#ref-Quillen1973Higher)] identify the boundary from $K_1(N;5)$ with this valuation map modulo five. We use the positive normalized valuation convention (Weibel 2013, Example V.6.1.2 and (V.6.6)) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. Thus $$K_1(N;5)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_vK_0(k_v;5)$$ is surjective.

<a id="eq:k4-restriction"></a>
The number field $N$ has $5$-cohomological dimension two (Weibel 2013, VI.§7 and VI.8.2) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. The odd-degree clause of Lemma <a href="#lem:bott" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott">4</a> therefore makes $b^2:K_1(N;5)\to K_5(N;5)$ an isomorphism. For every $v$, the finite field $k_v$ contains the primitive image of $\zeta$, so the finite-field clause makes $b^2:K_0(k_v;5)\to K_4(k_v;5)$ an isomorphism as well. The same restricted class $\beta$ acts on every term. Its compatibility with localization in Lemma <a href="#lem:bott-localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott-localization">3</a> carries the last surjection to a surjection $$K_5(N;5)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_vK_4(k_v;5).$$ In the exact localization segment $$K_5(N;5)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_vK_4(k_v;5)
 \longrightarrow K_4(S;5)\longrightarrow K_4(N;5),$$ the middle arrow is consequently zero. We obtain an injection $$\label{eq:k4-restriction}
 K_4(S;5)\hookrightarrow K_4(N;5).$$

We next determine where this injected group can lie. Take $x\in K_4(S;5)$ and write $x_N$ for its image. The even-degree clause of Lemma <a href="#lem:bott" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott">4</a> gives $b:K_2(N;5)\xrightarrow{\sim}K_4(N;5)$, so $x_N=b(z)$ for some $z\in K_2(N;5)$. The localization residues of $x_N$ in $K_3(k_v;5)$ vanish because $x$ extends over $S$. Write $\partial_v$ for the localization boundary at $v$. Compatibility with $b$ gives $$0=\partial_v(x_N)=b\bigl(\partial_v z\bigr).$$ Here $\partial_v z\in K_1(k_v;5)$, and $b:K_1(k_v;5)\to K_3(k_v;5)$ is injective by the finite-field clause. Thus every localization residue of $z$ is zero.

On the other hand, $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}z\in K_1(N)[5]=N^\times[5]=\langle\zeta\rangle$. Choose $m\in\mathbb F_5$ such that this boundary equals $m[\zeta]$, and consider the corrected class $z-m\beta_N$, where $\beta_N$ is the restriction of $\beta$. Equation <a href="#eq:equivariant-bott" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equivariant-bott">[eq:equivariant-bott]</a> makes its coefficient boundary zero. Its localization residues are still zero, since $\beta$ extends over $S$. Exactness of the coefficient sequence identifies this corrected class with an element of $K_2(N)/5$. Naturality of reduction with localization identifies its coefficient residues with the tame residues of that element. Lemma <a href="#lem:cyclotomic-unramified" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cyclotomic-unramified">5</a> therefore makes the corrected class zero. We have proved $$z=m\beta_N,\qquad x_N=m\,b(\beta_N).$$

<a id="eq:k4-geometric-injection"></a>
Let $\overline N$ be an algebraic closure of $N$. By the closed-field clause of Lemma <a href="#lem:bott" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott">4</a>, the restriction of $b(\beta_N)$ is the nonzero square of a geometric Bott generator in $K_4(\overline N;5)$. Restriction is therefore injective on the $\mathbb F_5$-line spanned by $b(\beta_N)$. Together with <a href="#eq:k4-restriction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:k4-restriction">[eq:k4-restriction]</a>, this proves $$\label{eq:k4-geometric-injection}
 K_4(S;5)\hookrightarrow K_4(\overline N;5).$$

It remains to pass from coefficient detection to integral torsion. The same closed-field clause gives $K_5(\overline N;5)=0$. The coefficient exact sequence then gives $K_4(\overline N)[5]=0$. In fact $K_4(\overline N)$ has no nonzero element of any finite five-power order: a suitable multiple of such an element would have order five.

Set $G=K_4(S)$, and write $\rho$ for coefficient reduction at each ring. Naturality gives the commutative square $$\begin{array}{ccc}
 G &\xrightarrow{\rho}& K_4(S;5)\\
 \big\downarrow && \big\downarrow\\
 K_4(\overline N)&\xrightarrow{\rho}&K_4(\overline N;5).
 \end{array}$$ The right vertical arrow is injective by <a href="#eq:k4-geometric-injection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:k4-geometric-injection">[eq:k4-geometric-injection]</a>. If $g\in G$ has finite five-power order, its integral image in $K_4(\overline N)$ is zero. The square therefore gives $\rho(g)=0$. Since $G/5$ injects into $K_4(S;5)$, this says $g\in5G$. This conclusion holds for every element of five-power order, not just for elements killed by five.

Finite generation now rules out a nonzero divisible primary subgroup. Quillen’s theorem makes $K_4(\mathcal O_N)$ finitely generated (Quillen 1973a, sec. 1, Theorem 1 and Remark (2)) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Quillen1973Finite](#ref-Quillen1973Finite)]; see also (Weibel 2013, 145:IV.6.9) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. Integral localization at the single place above five has the segment $$0=K_4(\mathbb F_5)\longrightarrow K_4(\mathcal O_N)
 \longrightarrow G\longrightarrow K_3(\mathbb F_5)=\mathbb Z/24.$$ The two finite-field values are Quillen’s integral computation (Quillen 1972) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[Quillen1972FiniteFields](#ref-Quillen1972FiniteFields)]; see (Weibel 2013, 145:IV.1.13) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[Weibel2013](#ref-Weibel2013)]. Thus $G$ is finitely generated. Write $G\simeq\mathbb Z^r\oplus P\oplus T$, where $P$ is its finite five-primary torsion subgroup and $T$ is finite of order prime to five. We proved $P\subseteq5G$. If $p=5g\in P$, the free component of $g$ is zero, and its $T$-component is zero because multiplication by five is an automorphism of $T$. Hence $g\in P$, and $P\subseteq5P$. The reverse inclusion is automatic. Iterating $P=5P$ to the exponent of the finite group $P$ gives $P=0$. In particular $K_4(S)[5]=0$. ◻

</details>

The coefficient obstruction has now been removed. We choose the degree-five class whose two Bott factors produce the square of the cyclotomic character.

The cyclotomic-unit/Bott pattern has a methodological predecessor in Soulé’s coefficient and tower constructions (Soulé 1987, secs. 4.2–4.4, pp. 239–240) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[Soule1987](#ref-Soule1987)]. The fixed integral $K_5(S)$-lift below is a separate deduction from $K_4(S)[5]=0$, proved here.

<a id="stmt-prop-integral-lift"></a>
<div id="prop:integral-lift" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:conjugate-c"></a>
**Proposition 7** (Integral lift and conjugates). *Let $u=\zeta-1\in S^\times$, and let $b$ be multiplication by the class $\beta$ in <a href="#eq:equivariant-bott" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equivariant-bott">[eq:equivariant-bott]</a>. There exists $c\in K_5(S)$ such that <a id="eq:integral-c"></a>$$\label{eq:integral-c}
 c\bmod5=b^2[u]_5\quad\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For every $\delta\in\Delta$, $$\label{eq:conjugate-c}
 (\delta c)\bmod5=\chi(\delta)^2b^2[\delta u]_5
       \quad\text{in }K_5(S;5).$$ For any fixed homomorphism $\iota:S\to H$ to a field, let $\operatorname{res}_\iota$ denote extension of scalars. Restriction of these equalities gives the corresponding identities in $K_5(H;5)$. In the second identity this means $$\operatorname{res}_\iota(\delta c)\bmod5
 =\chi(\delta)^2 b_\iota^2[\iota(\delta u)]_5,$$ where $b_\iota$ is multiplication by $\operatorname{res}_\iota(\beta)$; no action of $\delta$ on $H$ is required.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-integral-lift"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-integral-lift</summary>

*Proof.* Lemma <a href="#lem:cyclotomic-k4" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:cyclotomic-k4">6</a> and the coefficient exact sequence give $$0\longrightarrow K_5(S)/5\longrightarrow K_5(S;5)
 \longrightarrow K_4(S)[5]=0.$$ Consequently $K_5(S)\to K_5(S;5)$ is surjective, and $b^2[u]_5$ has an integral lift $c$. Applying $\delta$ after reduction, then using naturality and bilinearity of the parenthesized product, gives $$\begin{aligned}
 (\delta c)\bmod5
 &=\delta\bigl(\beta\cdot(\beta\cdot[u]_5)\bigr)\\
 &=(\delta\beta)\cdot\bigl((\delta\beta)\cdot[\delta u]_5\bigr)\\
 &=\chi(\delta)^2\beta\cdot(\beta\cdot[\delta u]_5).
 \end{aligned}$$ This proves <a href="#eq:conjugate-c" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conjugate-c">[eq:conjugate-c]</a>; functoriality gives its restriction along $\iota$. The construction made no equivariant choice of the integral lift $c$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:specialization"></a>
### A specialization criterion for supported classes

Our goal is to detect a sum of coefficient classes pushed forward from closed points of a curve. A vanishing sum would be a localization boundary. We first use the two Bott maps of Lemma <a href="#lem:bott" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bott">4</a> to express that boundary by ordinary tame symbols. The divisor boundary on a smooth model then imposes a relation on the special fiber, where a homomorphism from its Picard group can detect the contradiction.

<a id="stmt-prop-specialization"></a>
<div id="prop:specialization" class="proposition">

**Proposition 8** (Picard specialization). *Let $L$ be a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_5$ containing $\mu_5$, with valuation ring $R$ and residue field $k$. Let $\mathcal X\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$ be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension one with geometrically integral fibers $$C=\mathcal X_L,\qquad D=\mathcal X_k.$$ For a closed point $q$ of $C$, let $r(q)$ be its specialization on $D$: it is the image of the closed point under the unique extension of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\kappa(q)\to\mathcal X$ to the valuation ring of $\kappa(q)$. For a closed point $r$ of $D$, write $[r]\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(D)$ for the class of the divisor $(r)$. Let $m\geq0$ be an integer, and suppose that:*

1.  *$U$ is the spectrum of an arbitrary localization of the coordinate ring of a nonempty affine open of $C$, and $p_1,\ldots,p_m$ are distinct $L$-rational closed points of $U$;*

2.  *$Z$ is a finite set of closed points of $D$, and every closed point $q$ of $C$ with $r(q)\notin Z$ belongs to $U$;*

3.  *$B$ is an abelian group killed by $5$, and $\psi:\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(D)\to B$ is a homomorphism satisfying $\psi([r])=0$ for every $r\in Z$.*

<a id="eq:specialization-test"></a>
<a id="eq:specialization-conclusion"></a>
*Put $r_i=r(p_i)\in D(k)$. Choose $\beta_L\in K_2(L;5)$ with $\partial_{\mathrm{coef}}\beta_L=[\zeta]$ for a primitive fifth root of unity $\zeta$, and let $b$ be multiplication by its restrictions, with $b^2=b\circ b$. Let $v_L$ be the normalized valuation of $L$. For $a_i\in L^\times$ and $e_i\in\mathbb F_5$, the condition $$\label{eq:specialization-test}
 \sum_{i=1}^m e_i v_L(a_i)\,\psi([r_i])\ne0\quad\text{in }B$$ implies $$\label{eq:specialization-conclusion}
 \sum_{i=1}^m (p_i)_*\bigl(e_i b^2[a_i]_5\bigr)
       \ne0\quad\text{in }G_5(U;5).$$ Here $[a_i]_5$ is the class of $a_i$ in $K_1(L;5)=L^\times/L^{\times5}$, and the scalars in <a href="#eq:specialization-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:specialization-test">[eq:specialization-test]</a> act through the natural $\mathbb F_5$-module structure on $B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-specialization"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-specialization</summary>

*Proof.* The sum in <a href="#eq:specialization-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:specialization-test">[eq:specialization-test]</a> is zero when $m=0$, so its nonvanishing implies $m>0$. Suppose, for a contradiction, that the sum in <a href="#eq:specialization-conclusion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:specialization-conclusion">[eq:specialization-conclusion]</a> is zero. We first turn this assumed relation into prescribed ordinary tame symbols on the generic curve.

*Localization and Bott untwisting.* Put $J=L(C)$ and $A_U=\Gamma(U,\mathcal O_U)$. The ring $A_U$ is a localization of the coordinate ring of a smooth affine curve. It is therefore a Noetherian regular domain of dimension at most one, with fraction field $J$. Since one of the $p_i$ is retained, its dimension is one. Every finitely generated torsion $A_U$-module is killed by a nonzero element; the corresponding quotient of $A_U$ has dimension at most zero and is Noetherian, hence Artinian. Such a module has finite length, with simple factors the residue fields of the retained closed points. This argument does not require $U$ to be semilocal or the multiplicative set to be finite.

<a id="eq:curve-localization"></a>
Quillen localization for the torsion category, followed by dévissage and the coefficient convention in Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">2</a>, gives the exact sequence $$\label{eq:curve-localization}
 K_6(J;5)\longrightarrow
 \bigoplus_{q\in U^{(1)}}K_5(\kappa(q);5)
 \longrightarrow G_5(U;5).$$ The second arrow is the sum of coherent pushforwards from the points; the first is the vector of localization residues.
